, the fifth step follows from
\upsilon:=CR\tau
, the sixth step is straight forward, the seventh step follows from
\log(R)>1
,
C\geq\log(1/\delta_{1})
.

Hence, for any
t>0
,

\displaystyle H_{1}(t)=

\displaystyle~s_{0}\cdot\int_{t-0.5}^{t+0.5}\sinc(C_{0}R\cdot\tau)^{C\log R}\cdot\prod_{i=0}^{\log(S)}\sinc\big(\frac{C_{0}\cdot S}{2^{i}}\cdot\tau\big)^{2^{i}\cdot C}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~H_{1}(0)+s_{0}\cdot\int_{1}^{t+0.5}\sinc(C_{0}R\cdot\tau)^{C\log R}\cdot\prod_{i=0}^{\log(S)}\sinc\big(\frac{C_{0}\cdot S}{2^{i}}\cdot\tau\big)^{2^{i}\cdot C}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle~\quad-s_{0}\cdot\int_{-1}^{t-0.5}\sinc(C_{0}R\cdot\tau)^{C\log R}\cdot\prod_{i=0}^{\log(S)}\sinc\big(\frac{C_{0}\cdot S}{2^{i}}\cdot\tau\big)^{2^{i}\cdot C}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~H_{1}(0)-s_{0}\cdot\int_{-\infty}^{t-0.5}\sinc(C_{0}R\cdot\tau)^{C\log R}\cdot\prod_{i=0}^{\log(S)}\sinc\big(\frac{C_{0}\cdot S}{2^{i}}\cdot\tau\big)^{2^{i}\cdot C}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~H_{1}(0)-s_{0}O(\frac{1}{C_{0}^{2}R\log(R)})\delta_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~1-s_{0}O(\frac{1}{C_{0}^{2}R\log(R)})\delta_{1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~1-O(\delta_{1}),

where the first step follows from Eq. (
13
), the second step is straight forward, the third step follows from

\displaystyle\sinc(C_{0}R\cdot\tau)^{C\log R}\cdot\prod_{i=0}^{\log(S)}\sinc\big(\frac{C_{0}\cdot S}{2^{i}}\cdot\tau\big)^{2^{i}\cdot C}\geq 0,~~~\forall\tau\in\mathbb{R}

the forth step follows from Eq. (
16
), the fifth step follows from
H_{1}(0)=1
, the sixth step follows from Lemma
E.6
.

By re-scaling
\delta_{1}
, we get that
H(t)\geq 1-\delta_{1}
for any
|t|<0.5-\frac{\pi}{C_{0}R}
.

The lemma then follows from the upper and lower bounds.

∎

E.4
Energy preserving of the time domain filter

In this section, we show the properties of
H(t)
that we use in the rest of the paper.

We first prove Lemma
E.9
, which summarizes the results in above sections and prove the energy preserving property of
H(t)
.

Lemma E.9
.

The filter function
(H(t),\widehat{H}(f))
has the following properties:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Property~I}:

\displaystyle|H(t)|\leq 1.01,~\forall t\in\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Property~II}:

\displaystyle 1-\delta_{1}\leq H(t)\leq 1,~\forall|t|<\alpha^{-1}(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\pi}{CR})

\displaystyle\mathrm{Property~III}:

\displaystyle|\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{H}(f))|\leq O(k^{2}\log^{2}(k)\log^{2}(1/\delta_{1}))

\displaystyle\mathrm{Property~IV}:

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\bigl|x^{*}(t)\cdot H(t)\cdot(1-\mathrm{rect}_{2}(t))\bigr|^{2}\mathrm{d}t<0.1\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|x^{*}(t)\cdot\mathrm{rect}_{2}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\mathrm{Property~V}:

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|x^{*}(t)\cdot H(t)\cdot\mathrm{rect}_{2}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\in[0.9,1.1]\cdot\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|x^{*}(t)\cdot\mathrm{rect}_{2}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

Proof.

We prove each of the five properties in below.

Property I:

By Theorem
E.4
Part 3, we have that

\displaystyle|H(t)|\leq 1.01.

Property II:

By Lemma
E.8
, we have that

\displaystyle 1-\delta_{1}\leq H(t)\leq 1,~\forall|t|<\alpha^{-1}(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\pi}{CR}).

Property III:

By the
k
-Fourier-sparse signals’ energy bound (Theorem
B.1
), we have that

\displaystyle R=O(k^{2}).

By Theorem
E.1
, we have that

\displaystyle S=O(k^{2}\log k).

Then, by Lemma
E.3
, we have that

\displaystyle|\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{H}(f))|=

\displaystyle~C^{2}R\log R+C^{2}S\log S

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~O(\log(1/\delta_{1}))^{2}\cdot O(k^{2}\log k\log(k^{2}\log k))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~O(k^{2}\log^{2}k\log^{2}(1/\delta_{1})).

Property IV:

We have that

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\bigl|x^{*}(t)\cdot H(t)\cdot(1-\mathrm{rect}_{1}(t))\bigr|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\bigl|x^{*}(t)\cdot H(t)\bigr|^{2}\mathrm{d}t-\int_{-1}^{1}\bigl|x^{*}(t)\cdot H(t)\bigr|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~0.06\int_{-1}^{+1}\bigl|x^{*}(t)\cdot H(t)\bigr|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~0.1\int_{-1}^{+1}\bigl|x^{*}(t)\bigr|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~0.1\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|x^{*}(t)\cdot\mathrm{rect}_{1}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t,

where the first step is straight forward, the second step follows from Theorem
E.4
Part 2, the third step follows from Theorem
E.4
Part 3, the forth step is straight forward.

Property V:

We first prove the upper bound:

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|x^{*}(t)\cdot H(t)\cdot\mathrm{rect}_{1}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\int_{-1}^{+1}|x^{*}(t)\cdot H(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~1.1\int_{-1}^{+1}|x^{*}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~1.1\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|x^{*}(t)\cdot\mathrm{rect}_{1}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t,

where the first step is straight forward, the second step follows from Theorem
E.4
Part 3, the third step is straight forward.

Then, we prove the lower bound:

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|x^{*}(t)\cdot H(t)\cdot\mathrm{rect}_{1}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\int_{-1}^{+1}|x^{*}(t)\cdot H(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~0.9\cdot\int_{-1}^{+1}|x^{*}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~0.9\cdot\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|x^{*}(t)\cdot\mathrm{rect}_{1}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

where the first step is straight forward, the second step follows from Theorem
E.4
Part 1, the third step is straight forward.

∎

The following lemma
bounds the length of the fluctuation region (where
H(t)
is close to
1
) in the time domain.

Lemma E.10
.

Let
\Delta=k|\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{H}(f))|
,
\beta=O(1/\Delta)
,

L=\frac{T}{2}-\alpha^{-1}(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\pi}{C_{0}R})\frac{T}{2},R=\frac{T}{2}+\alpha^{-1}(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\pi}{C_{0}R})\frac{T}{2}-\beta
,
we have that

\displaystyle T-k^{2}(T+L-R)\eqsim T,

and

\displaystyle R-L\eqsim T.

Proof.

Let
U:=[L,R]
. By Lemma
E.8
, we have that for any
t_{0}\in U
,

\displaystyle H(t)>1-\delta_{1},\forall t\in[t_{0},t_{0}+\beta].

We have that

\displaystyle R-L=

\displaystyle~|U|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~((\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1.2}{\pi CR})^{-1}\cdot(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\pi}{CR})-\beta)\cdot T

\displaystyle\eqsim

\displaystyle~T,

where the first step follows from the definition of
L,R
, the second step follows from Lemma
E.9
Property II, the third step follows from
\Delta,CR\gg 1
.

We have that

\displaystyle T+L-R=

\displaystyle~T-|U|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(1-(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1.2}{\pi CR})^{-1}\cdot(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\pi}{CR}))\cdot T+\beta T

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\frac{2\pi+{2.4}/{\pi}}{CR+{2.4}/{\pi}}\cdot T+\beta T,

(17)

where the first step follows from the definition of
L,R
, the second step follows from Lemma
E.9
Property II, the third step is straight forward.

Then, we have that

\displaystyle T-k^{2}(T+L-R)=T-k^{2}\cdot(\frac{2\pi+{2.4}/{\pi}}{CR+{2.4}/{\pi}}+\beta)\cdot T\eqsim T,

where the first step follows from Eq. (
17
), the second step follows from
C=O(\log(1/\delta_{1}))
,
R=k^{2}
,
k^{2}\beta<1/k
.

∎

Appendix F
Ideal Filter Approximation

As we discussed in previous sections, the filtered signal
z^{(j)}(t)=(x\cdot H)*G_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}(t)
is the signal in the
j
-th bin by the
HashToBins
procedure. In this section, we consider an approximation of the frequency domain filter
G_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
by the
ideal filter

I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}(t)
defined by its Fourier transform:

\displaystyle\widehat{I}_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}(f):=\begin{cases}1,&~\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(f)>1-\delta_{1}\\
0,&~\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

(18)

Intuitively,
I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
is “denoising” the frequency domain filter
\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
in the sense that it rounds the heavy Fourier coefficients of
\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
to 1 and rounds the remaining Fourier coefficients to 0. The main purpose of this section is to show that
(x\cdot H)*I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
is a good approximation of
z^{(j)}
. For simplicity, we will use
I
to denote
I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
when
\sigma,b,j
are clear from context.

We first show a commuting property of the ideal filter (see Section
F.1
). Then, we derive the approximation error bound of the ideally filtered signals (see Section
F.2
).

F.1
Swap the order of filtering

We first prove a good property of the ideal filter that
I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
“commutes” with the time domain filter
H
with high probability over the random hashing function.

Lemma F.1
.

Let
\delta_{1}
be the
\delta
defined in Lemma
C.4
.Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
. Let the ideal filter
I=I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
be defined as in Eq. (
18
).

Then, for any
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
, with probability
0.9
over the choice of
(\sigma,b)
, for any
j\in[B]
,

\displaystyle(x\cdot H)*I(t)=(x*I)(t)\cdot H(t)~~~\forall t\in\mathbb{R}.

Proof.

By Fourier transformation, we have

\displaystyle(x\cdot H)*I(t)=

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})(f)\cdot\widehat{I}(f)\cdot\exp(2\pi\mathbf{i}ft)\mathrm{d}f.

We will show that
(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})(f)\cdot\widehat{I}(f)=((\widehat{x}\cdot\widehat{I})*\widehat{H})(f)
with high probability.

On the one hand,

\displaystyle(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})(f)\cdot\widehat{I}(f)=

\displaystyle~\sum_{j=1}^{k}v_{j}\cdot(\delta_{f_{j}}*\widehat{H})(f)\cdot\widehat{I}(f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{j=1}^{k}v_{j}\cdot\widehat{H}(f-f_{j})\cdot\widehat{I}(f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{j=1}^{k}v_{j}\cdot\widehat{H}(f-f_{j})\cdot{\bf 1}_{f\in\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{I})},

where the first step follows from
\widehat{x}(f)=\sum_{j=1}^{k}v_{j}\cdot\delta_{f_{j}}(f)
, the second step follows from the convolution property of Delta function. By Lemma
D.8
, we get that with probability at least
0.9
, for any
j\in[k]
and any
f\in\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{H})+f_{j}
, either
\widehat{G}_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}<\delta_{1}
or
\widehat{G}_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}>1-\delta_{1}
. In other words, either
f_{j}+\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{H})\subseteq\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{I})
or
f_{j}+\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{H})\cap\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{I})=\emptyset
. Since
0\in\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{H})
, we get that for any
f\in f_{j}+\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{H})
,

\displaystyle f\in\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{I})~~\Longleftrightarrow~~f_{j}\in\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{I}).

Hence, we have

\displaystyle(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})(f)\cdot\widehat{I}(f)=\sum_{j\in[k]:f_{j}\in\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{I})}v_{j}\cdot\widehat{H}(f-f_{j}).

On the other hand,

\displaystyle(\widehat{x}\cdot\widehat{I})*\widehat{H}(f)=

\displaystyle~\sum_{j\in[k]:f_{j}\in\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{I})}v_{j}\cdot\delta_{f_{j}}*\widehat{H}(f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{j\in[k]:f_{j}\in\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{I})}v_{j}\cdot\widehat{H}(f-f_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})(f)\cdot\widehat{I}(f).

Therefore,

\displaystyle(x\cdot H)*I(t)=

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})(f)\cdot\widehat{I}(f)\cdot\exp(2\pi\mathbf{i}ft)\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\widehat{x}\cdot\widehat{I})*\widehat{H}(f)\cdot\exp(2\pi\mathbf{i}ft)\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(x*I)(t)\cdot H(t),

where the last step follows from the definition of Fourier transform.

The lemma is then proved.

∎

F.2
Approximation error bounds

We analyze the approximation error due to replacing
\widehat{G}_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
with the ideal filter
I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
defined by Eq. (
18
). The following lemma gives a point-wise error bound.

Lemma F.2
.

Let
\delta_{1}
be defined as in Lemma
C.4
. Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
.

For any
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
, we have that with probability
0.9
, for any
j\in[B]
,

\displaystyle|(x\cdot H)*{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)-(x\cdot H)*I(t)|\lesssim\delta_{1}\sqrt{T|S|}\cdot\|x(t)\|_{T}~~~\forall t\in\mathbb{R}.

Proof.

Let
S:=\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})
be defined as the support set of
\widehat{x}*\widehat{H}
.
Then
|S|\leq\Delta
.

We have that

\displaystyle~|(x\cdot H)*{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)-(x\cdot H)*I(t)|

(19)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~|(x\cdot H)*({G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}-I)(t)|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\Big|\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})(f)\cdot(\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}-\widehat{I})(f)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}ft}\mathrm{d}f\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})(f)\cdot(\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}-I)(f)|\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\int_{S}|(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})(f)\cdot(\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}-I)(f)|\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\int_{S}|(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})(f)\cdot\delta_{1}|\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}\sqrt{|S|}\cdot\sqrt{\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}\sqrt{|S|}\cdot\sqrt{\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|(x\cdot H)(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}\sqrt{T|S|}\cdot\|(x\cdot H)(t)\|_{T}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}\sqrt{T|S|}\cdot\|x(t)\|_{T}

(20)

where the first step is straight forward, the second step follows from the definition of Fourier transform, the third step follows from triangle equality, the forth step follows from the definition of
S
. For the fifth step, by Lemma
D.8
that with probability at least 0.9, the Large Offset event does not happen (i.e., for any
f\in S
, either
\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(f)<\delta_{1}
or
\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(f)>1-\delta_{1}
). Then, by Lemma
C.4
, we know that
-\delta_{1}\leq\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(f)\leq 1
. Thus, we get that
|(\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}-\widehat{I})(f)|\leq\delta_{1}
. The sixth step follows from Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, the seventh step follows from Parseval’s theorem, the eighth step follows from Lemma
E.9
Property IV and V,
the last step follows from Lemma
E.9
Property V.
∎

The following lemma gives a
T
-norm bound for the approximation error.

Lemma F.3
.

Let
\delta_{1}
be defined as in Lemma
C.4
. Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
.

Then, for any
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
, with probability
0.9
, for any
j\in[B]
,

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|(x\cdot H)*I(t)-(x\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\delta_{1}^{2}T\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}.

In particular,

\displaystyle\|(x\cdot H)*I(t)-(x\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)\|_{T}\lesssim\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{T}.

Proof.

Let
S:=\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})
be defined as the support set of
\widehat{x}*\widehat{H}
.

We have that

\displaystyle~T\|(x\cdot H)*I(t)-(x\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\int_{0}^{T}|(x\cdot H)*I(t)-(x\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|(x\cdot H)*I(t)-(x\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})(f)\cdot(\widehat{I}(f)-\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(f))|^{2}\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\int_{S}|(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})(f)\cdot(I(f)-\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(f))|^{2}\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\int_{S}|(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})(f)\cdot\delta_{1}|^{2}\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})(f)\cdot\delta_{1}|^{2}\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}^{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|(x\cdot H)(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}^{2}T\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}

where the first step follows from the definition of the norm, the second step is straight forward, the third step follows from Parseval’s theorem, the forth step follows from the definition of
S
, the fifth step follows from Lemma
D.8
and Lemma
C.4
, the sixth step is straight forward, the seventh step follows from Parseval’s theorem, the eighth step follows from Lemma
E.9
Property IV and Property V.

∎

Appendix G
Concentration Property of the Filtered Signal

Recall that a frequency
f^{*}
is
heavy
if it satisfies the following condition:

\displaystyle\int_{f^{*}-\Delta_{h}}^{f^{*}+\Delta_{h}}|\widehat{x^{*}\cdot H}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f\geq T\mathcal{N}^{2}/k.

In this section, we consider the filtered signal in a hashing bin that contains a heavy frequency; that is,
z(t)=(x^{*}\cdot H)*\widehat{G}_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
where
j=h_{\sigma,b}(f^{*})
is the index of the bin containing
f^{*}
. We will prove that
z(t)
form a one-cluster signal around
f^{*}
, which means that in the frequency domain most energy are concentrated around
f^{*}
, and in the time domain, most energy are contained in the observation window
[0,T]
. The formal definition are given as follows:

Definition G.1

(
(\varepsilon,\Delta)
-one-cluster signal)
.

We say that a signal
z(t)
is an
(\varepsilon,\Delta)
-one-cluster signal around
f_{0}
if and only if
z(t)
and
\widehat{z}(f)
satisfy the following two properties:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Property~I}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\int_{f_{0}-\Delta}^{f_{0}+\Delta}|\widehat{z}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f\geq(1-\varepsilon)\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|\widehat{z}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle\mathrm{Property~II}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\geq(1-\varepsilon)\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t.

We first prove the energy preservation in the time domain:

Lemma G.2

(Time domain energy preservation)
.

Let
\Delta_{h}=|\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{H})|
. Let
f^{*}
satisfy

\displaystyle\int_{f^{*}-\Delta_{h}}^{f^{*}+\Delta_{h}}|\widehat{x^{*}\cdot H}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f\geq T\mathcal{N}^{2}/k

and
\widehat{z}=\widehat{x^{*}\cdot H}\cdot\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
where
j=h_{\sigma,b}(f^{*})
.
Suppose the Large Offset event does not happen.
Then, we have that,

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\leq 1.35\int_{0}^{T}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t.

Proof.

Let
I(f)
be the ideal filter defined by Eq. (
18
).

We first have

\displaystyle\|z(t)\|_{L_{2}}=

\displaystyle~\|(x^{*}\cdot H)(t)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)\|_{L_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\|(x^{*}\cdot H)(t)*I(t)\|_{L_{2}}+\|(x^{*}\cdot H)(t)*(I-G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b})(t)\|_{L_{2}},

where the second step follows from triangle inequality.

Then, we bound the two terms separately.

For the first term, by Lemma
F.1
, if the Large Offset event does not happen, we have that

\displaystyle(x^{*}\cdot H)*I(t)=(x^{*}*I)(t)\cdot H(t).

(21)

It implies that

\displaystyle\|(x^{*}\cdot H)(t)*I(t)\|_{L_{2}}=

\displaystyle~\|(x^{*}*I)(t)\cdot H(t)\|_{L_{2}}

Let
y(t):=(x^{*}*I)(t)
. It’s easy to see that
y(y)
is
k
-Fourier-sparse. Then, we have

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|y(t)\cdot H(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\int_{0}^{T}|y(t)\cdot H(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t+\int_{[-\infty,\infty]\backslash[0,T]}|y(t)\cdot H(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\int_{0}^{T}|y(t)\cdot H(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t+0.1\int_{0}^{T}|y(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~1.1\int_{0}^{T}|y(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~1.3\int_{0}^{T}|y(t)\cdot H(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t,

(22)

where the first step is straight forward, the second step follows from Lemma
E.9
Property IV, the third step follows from Lemma
E.9
Property V, the forth step follows from Lemma
E.9
Property V.
Hence,

\displaystyle\|(x^{*}\cdot H)(t)*I(t)\|_{L_{2}}=\|y(t)\cdot H(t)\|_{L_{2}}\leq\sqrt{1.3T}\cdot\|(x^{*}*I)(t)\cdot H(t)\|_{T}.

By Eq. (
21
) again, we can swap the order of
I
and
H
and obtain:

\displaystyle\|(x^{*}\cdot H)(t)*I(t)\|_{L_{2}}\leq\sqrt{1.3T}\cdot\|(x^{*}\cdot H)(t)*I(t)\|_{T}.

For the second term, by Lemma
F.3
, we have that

\displaystyle\|(x^{*}\cdot H)*I(t)-(x^{*}\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)\|_{L_{2}}\lesssim\delta_{1}\sqrt{T}\|x^{*}(t)\|_{T}.

(23)

Therefore, we get that

\displaystyle\|z(t)\|_{L_{2}}\leq

\displaystyle~\sqrt{1.3T}\cdot\|(x^{*}\cdot H)(t)*I(t)\|_{T}+O(\delta_{1}\sqrt{T}\|x^{*}(t)\|_{T})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sqrt{1.3T}\cdot\|(x^{*}\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)\|_{T}+\sqrt{1.3T}\cdot\|(x^{*}\cdot H)*(I-G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b})(t)\|_{T}+O(\delta_{1}\sqrt{T}\|x^{*}(t)\|_{T})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\sqrt{1.3T}\cdot\|z(t)\|_{T}+O(\delta_{1}\sqrt{T}\|x^{*}(t)\|_{T}),

where the second step follows from triangle inequality, and the last step follows from Lemma
F.3
again.

We claim that the second term can be bounded by
o(1)\cdot\|z(t)\|_{T}
.
Indeed, we have

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|(x^{*}\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|(\widehat{x}^{*}*\widehat{H})\cdot\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\int_{f^{*}-\Delta_{h}}^{f^{*}+\Delta_{h}}|(\widehat{x}^{*}*\widehat{H})\cdot\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle~\int_{f^{*}-\Delta_{h}}^{f^{*}+\Delta_{h}}|(\widehat{x}^{*}*\widehat{H})(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle~\int_{f^{*}-\Delta_{h}}^{f^{*}+\Delta_{h}}|(\widehat{x}^{*}*\widehat{H})(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\frac{T\delta\|x^{*}\|_{T}^{2}}{k}

(24)

where the first step follows from Parseval’s theorem, the second step is straightforward, the third step follows from Lemma
D.8
and Lemma
C.4
Property I,
the forth step follows from our assumption that there exists a heavy frequency
f^{*}
hashing into the
j
-th bin, the fifth step follows from
f^{*}
satisfying

\displaystyle\int_{f^{*}-\Delta}^{f^{*}+\Delta}|\widehat{x^{*}\cdot H}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f\geq T\mathcal{N}^{2}/k.

Thus,
\|x^{*}\|_{T}\leq O(\sqrt{k/(T\delta)})\|z(t)\|_{L_{2}}
and we have

\displaystyle O(\delta_{1}\sqrt{T}\|x^{*}\|_{T})=O\Big(\delta_{1}\sqrt{\frac{k}{\delta}}\|z(t)\|_{L_{2}}\Big)\leq O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{\delta}{k}}\Big)\|z(t)\|_{L_{2}}=o(1)\cdot\|z(t)\|_{L_{2}},

where the second step follows from
\delta_{1}\leq\delta/k
.

Finally, we have

\displaystyle\|z(t)\|_{L_{2}}\leq\sqrt{1.3T}\cdot\|z(t)\|_{T}+o(1)\cdot\|z(t)\|_{L_{2}},

which implies that

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\leq 1.35\int_{0}^{T}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t.

The lemma is then proved.

∎

We next show the frequency domain energy concentration in the following lemma. Together with Lemma
G.2
, we conclude that
z(t)
is a one-cluster signal.

Lemma G.3

(Frequency domain energy concentration)
.

Let
x^{*}
be a
k
-Fourier-sparse signal. Let
f^{*}\in[-F,F]
satisfy the following property:

\int_{f^{*}-\Delta_{h}}^{f^{*}+\Delta_{h}}|\widehat{x^{*}\cdot H}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f\geq T\mathcal{N}^{2}/k.

(25)

Let
\sigma,b
be the parameter of the hashing function. Suppose that Large Offset event not happened and
f^{*}
is well-isolated. Let
j=h_{\sigma,b}(f^{*})
be the bucket that
f^{*}
maps to under the hash such that
z=(x^{*}\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
and
\widehat{z}=\widehat{x^{*}\cdot H}\cdot\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\int_{f^{*}-\Delta}^{f^{*}+\Delta}|\widehat{z}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f\geq 0.7\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|\widehat{z}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f.

Furthermore,
z(t)
is a
(0.3,\Delta)
-one-cluster signal around
f^{*}
.

Proof.

Define region
I_{f^{*}}=(f^{*}-\Delta,f^{*}+\Delta)
with the complement
\overline{I_{f^{*}}}=(-\infty,\infty)\setminus I_{f^{*}}
. We have that

\displaystyle\int_{I_{f^{*}}}|\widehat{z}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f\geq(1-\delta/k)\int_{I_{f^{*}}}|\widehat{x^{*}\cdot H}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f\geq(1-o(1))T\mathcal{N}^{2}/k

where the first step follows from Lemma
D.9
,
the second step follows from Eq. (
25
).

On the other hand,
f^{*}
is well-isolated. Thus, by the definition of well-isolation (Definition
D.4
), we have that

\int_{\overline{I_{f^{*}}}}|\widehat{z}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f\lesssim\varepsilon\cdot T\mathcal{N}^{2}/k\leq 0.1T\mathcal{N}^{2}/k.

Combining them together, we get that

\displaystyle\int_{f_{0}-\Delta}^{f_{0}+\Delta}|\widehat{z}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f\geq 0.7\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|\widehat{z}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f

For the furthermore part, Lemma
G.2
implies that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\geq(1-0.3)\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t.

Hence, by Definition
G.1
,
z(t)
is a
(0.3,\Delta)
-one-cluster.
∎

Appendix H
Energy Bound for Filtered Fourier Sparse Signals

In this section, we prove an energy bound for the filtered signals
(x\cdot H)*G_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
, which upper bounds the magnitude of any such signal at a point
t
by its energy in the time duration
[0,T]
. We first prove an energy bound for untruncated ideally filtered signals (see Section
H.1
). Then, we prove an energy bound for filtered signals (see Section
H.2
). In addition, we prove a technical claim (see Section
H.3
).

H.1
Energy bound for untruncated ideally filtered signals

In Section
F
, we show that the ideally filtered signal
(x\cdot H)*I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
, where
I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
defined as Eq. (
18
) is the ideal filter, is close to the true filtered signal. Here, we further simplify the signal by ignoring the truncation filter
H(t)
, and prove an energy bound for the signals of the form
(x*I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)})(t)
:

Lemma H.1
.

Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
and the corresponding ideal filter
I=I_{\sigma,b}^{(b)}
be defined as in Eq. (
18
).
Let
D(t):=\mathrm{Uniform}([-1,1])
.

For any
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
, we have that with probability
0.6
, for any
t\in(-1,1)

\displaystyle|(x*I)(t)|^{2}\lesssim{\min}\Big\{\frac{k}{1-|t|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot\|(x*I)(t)\|_{D}^{2}

Proof.

Since
\widehat{(x*I)}(f)=\widehat{x}\cdot\widehat{I}(f)
and
x
is
k
-Fourier-sparse, we know that
(x*\widehat{I})(t)
is also a
k
-Fourier-sparse signal.

On the one hand, by the
k
-Fourier-sparse signal’s location-dependent energy bound (Theorem
B.2
), we have

\displaystyle|(x*I)(t)|^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle~\frac{k}{1-|t|}\|(x*I)(t)\|_{D}^{2}

(26)

On the other hand, by the location-independent energy bound (Theorem
B.1
), we have that

\displaystyle|(x*I)(t)|^{2}\lesssim k^{2}\|(x*I)(t)\|_{D}^{2}

(27)

Combine Eq. (
26
) and Eq. (
27
) together, we prove the lemma:

\displaystyle|(x*I)(t)|^{2}\lesssim{\min}\Big\{\frac{k}{1-|t|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot\|(x*I)(t)\|_{D}^{2}.

∎

H.2
Energy bound for filtered signals

Based on Lemma
H.1
, we can relate the magnitude of the filtered signal with its own energy plus the original Fourier-sparse signal’s energy.

Lemma H.2
.

Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
and the corresponding ideal filter
I=I_{\sigma,b}^{(b)}
be defined as in Eq. (
18
).

For any
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
,
j\in[B]
, and
(\sigma,b)
such that Large Offset event does not happen, let
z(t)=(x\cdot H)*{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)
. It holds that:

\displaystyle{|z(t)|^{2}}\lesssim{\min}\Big\{\frac{k\cdot H(t)}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot{\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}}+\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}~~~\forall t\in(-1,1).

Proof.

Let
S:=\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})
be defined as the support set of
\widehat{x}*\widehat{H}
.
Then
|S|\leq\Delta
.

First, by the ideally untruncated filtered signal’s energy bound (Lemma
H.1
), we have

\displaystyle|(x*I)(t)\cdot H(t)|^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle~H^{2}(t)\cdot{\min}\Big\{\frac{k}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot\|(x*I)(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k\cdot H(t)}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot\|(x*I)(t)\|_{T}^{2},

(28)

where the second step follows from
H(t)\lesssim 1
(Lemma
E.9
Property I, II).

Then, we bound the magnitude of the ideal filtered signal as follows:

\displaystyle|(x\cdot H)*I(t)|^{2}=

\displaystyle~|(x*I)(t)\cdot H(t)|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k\cdot H(t)}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot\|(x*I)(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k\cdot H(t)}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot(\|(x\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)\|_{T}^{2}+\delta_{1}^{2}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k\cdot H(t)}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot\|(x\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)\|_{T}^{2}+\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}

(29)

where the first step follows from Lemma
F.1
, the second step follows from Eq. (
28
), the third step follows from Claim
H.5
,
the forth step follows from
k^{2}\delta_{1}\leq 1
.

Next, we consider the difference between the signals filtered by
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)
and
I(t)
:

\displaystyle|(x\cdot H)*{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)-(x\cdot H)*I(t)|^{2}\leq

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}^{2}{T|S|}\cdot\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}\cdot\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}

(30)

where the first step follows from Lemma
F.2
, the second step follows from
\delta_{1}T|S|\leq 1
.

Finally, we have that

\displaystyle|(x\cdot H)*{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)|^{2}\leq

\displaystyle~2|(x\cdot H)*I(t)|^{2}+2|(x\cdot H)*{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)-(x\cdot H)*I(t)|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~|(x\cdot H)*I(t)|^{2}+\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k\cdot H(t)}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot\|(x\cdot H)*\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)\|_{T}^{2}+\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2},

where the first step follows from
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
, the second step follows from Eq. (
30
), the third step follows from Eq. (
29
).

The lemma is then proved.

∎

The energy bound in Lemma
H.2
not only depends on
\|z(t)\|_{T}
, but also on
\|x(t)\|_{T}
. The following lemma show that assuming the filtered signal contains a heavy frequency,
\|x(t)\|_{T}
can be upper bounded by
\|z(t)\|_{T}
.

Lemma H.3
.

Given
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},F\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
. Let
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
be any
k
-Fourier sparse signal.
For
j\in[B]
such that there exists a
f^{*}
satisfying:
j=h_{\sigma,b}(f^{*})
and

\displaystyle\int_{f^{*}-\Delta_{h}}^{f^{*}+\Delta_{h}}|\widehat{x\cdot H}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f\geq T\mathcal{N}^{2}/k,

(31)

where
\mathcal{N}^{2}\geq\delta\|x\|_{T}^{2}
and
\Delta_{h}=|\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{H})|
.

For any
(\sigma,b)
that Large Offset event does not happen, we have that

\displaystyle\|(x\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)\|^{2}_{T}\gtrsim\frac{\delta\|x\|_{T}^{2}}{k}.

Proof.

We have that

\displaystyle T\|(x\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)\|^{2}_{T}=

\displaystyle~\int_{0}^{T}|(x\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|(x\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})\cdot\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\int_{f^{*}-\Delta_{h}}^{f^{*}+\Delta_{h}}|(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})\cdot\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle~\int_{f^{*}-\Delta_{h}}^{f^{*}+\Delta_{h}}|(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~\frac{T\delta\|x\|_{T}^{2}}{k}

where the first step follows from the definition of norm, the second step follows from Lemma
G.3
, the third step follows from Parseval’s theorem, the forth step is straight forward,
the fifth step follows from Lemma
D.9
,
the sixth step follows from Eq. (
31
).

∎

Lemma
H.2
and Lemma
H.3
implies the following energy bound:

Corollary H.4

(Energy bound for filtered signals)
.

Given
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
F\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Let
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
.
Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
with
(\sigma,b)
such that Large Offset event does not happen.

For any
j\in[B]
, suppose there exists an
f^{*}
with
j=h_{\sigma,b}(f^{*})
satisfying:

\displaystyle\int_{f^{*}-\Delta}^{f^{*}+\Delta}|\widehat{x\cdot H}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f\geq T\mathcal{N}^{2}/k,

where
\mathcal{N}^{2}\geq\delta\|x\|_{T}^{2}
.
Then, for
z(t)=(x\cdot H)*{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)
, it holds that:

\displaystyle{|z(t)|^{2}}\lesssim{\min}\Big\{\frac{k\cdot H(t)+\delta}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot{\|z(t)\|_{D}^{2}}~~~\forall t\in(0,T).

Proof.

We have that

\displaystyle|z(t)|^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k\cdot H(t)}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}+\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k\cdot H(t)}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}+\delta^{2}k^{-1}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k\cdot H(t)}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}+\delta\|(x\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k\cdot H(t)+\delta}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}

where the first step follows from Lemma
H.2
, the second step follows from
\delta_{1}\leq\delta^{2}k^{-1}
, the third step follows from Lemma
H.3
, the forth step is straight forward.

∎

H.3
Technical claim

Claim H.5
.

Given
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},F\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Let
\delta_{1}
be defined as the
\delta
of Lemma
C.4
.Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
, and
I=I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
be the ideal filter defined by Eq. (
18
).

Then, for any
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
and
j\in[B]
, with probability
0.6
over
(\sigma,b)
, we have that

\displaystyle\|(x*I)(t)\|_{T}^{2}\lesssim\|(x\cdot H)*\widehat{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)\|_{T}^{2}+\delta_{1}^{2}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}.

Proof.

We have that

\displaystyle\|(x*I)(t)\|_{T}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle~\|(x*I)(t)\cdot H\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\|(x\cdot H)*I(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~2\|(x\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)\|_{T}^{2}+2\|(x\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)-(x\cdot H)*I(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\|(x\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)\|_{T}^{2}+\delta_{1}^{2}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}

where the first step follows from
(x*I)(t)
is a
k
-Fourier-sparse signal and Lemma
E.9
Property V, the second step follows from Lemma
F.1
conditioning on Large Offset event not happening, the third step follows from
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
, the forth step follows from Lemma
F.3
.
∎

Appendix I
Local-Test Signal

Recall that the filtered signal in the
j
-th bin of the
HashToBins
procedure can be written as
z(t)=(x\cdot H)*G_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}(t)
. The next step of the frequency estimation algorithm is to extract a significant frequency from
z(t)
by considering a so-called
local-test signal
:

\displaystyle d_{z}(t):=z(t)e^{2\pi f_{0}\beta}-z(t+\beta),

(32)

where
f_{0}\in\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{x}^{*})
, and
j=h_{\sigma,b}(f_{0})
, where
\beta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
is a parameter such that
\beta\leq O(1/\Delta)
with
\Delta=O(k\cdot|\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{H})|)
.

In this section, we will study some properties of
d_{z}(t)
and its ideal versions (see Section
I.1
and Section
I.2
) and derive an energy bound for it (See Section
I.3
).

I.1
Ideal local-test signal

In previous section, we’ve shown that ideal filter
I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
can be used to approximate
G_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
such that the ideally filtered signal is close to the true filtered signal. We will show that under the ideal filter approximation, the
ideal local-test signal
is also close to the true local-test signal. More formally, we define the ideal filtered signal and the ideal local-test signal as follows:

\displaystyle z_{I}(t):=

\displaystyle~(x\cdot H)*I(t),

\displaystyle d_{I,z}(t):=

\displaystyle~z_{I}(t)e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-z_{I}(t+\beta),

(33)

The following lemma bounds the point-wise distance between
d_{z}(t)
and
d_{I,z}(t)
.

Lemma I.1
.

Let
\delta_{1}
be defined as in Lemma
C.4
. Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
and
I=I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
be the corresponding ideal filter as in Eq. (
18
).

For any
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
and
(\sigma,b)
such that Large Offset event does not happen, for any
j\in[B]
, let
z(t)=(x\cdot H)*G_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}(t)
,
d_{z}(t)
be defined as Eq. (
32
),
z_{I}(t)
and
d_{z,I}(t)
be defined as Eq. (
I.1
).

Then, we have

\displaystyle|d_{z}(t)-d_{I,z}(t)|\lesssim\delta_{1}\sqrt{T|S|}\cdot\|x(t)\|_{T}~~~\forall t\in\mathbb{R}.

Proof.

\displaystyle|d_{z}(t)-d_{I,z}(t)|=

\displaystyle~|z(t)e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-z_{I}(t)e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-(z(t+\beta)-z_{I}(t+\beta))|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~|z(t)e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-z_{I}(t)e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}|+|z(t+\beta)-z_{I}(t+\beta)|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~|z(t)-z_{I}(t)|+|z(t+\beta)-z_{I}(t+\beta)|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}\sqrt{T|S|}\cdot\|x(t)\|_{T},

where the first step follows from the definition of
d_{z}(t)
and
d_{I,z}(t)
, the second step follows from triangle inequality, the third step follows from
|e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}|=1
, the forth step follows from Lemma
F.2
.
∎

The following lemma bounds the
L_{2}
-distance between
d_{z}(t)
and
d_{z,I}(t)
.

Lemma I.2
.

Let
\delta_{1}
be defined as in Lemma
C.4
. Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
and
I=I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
be the corresponding ideal filter as in Eq. (
18
).

For any
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
and
(\sigma,b)
such that Large Offset event does not happen, for any
j\in[B]
, let
z(t)=(x\cdot H)*G_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}(t)
,
d_{z}(t)
be defined as Eq. (
32
),
z_{I}(t)
and
d_{z,I}(t)
be defined as Eq. (
I.1
).Then,

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|d_{I,z}(t)-d_{z}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\delta_{1}^{2}T\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}

Proof.

We first have that,

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|z_{I}(t)e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-z(t)e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|z_{I}(t)-z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}^{2}T\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2},

(34)

where the first step follows from
|e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}|=1
, the second step follows from Lemma
F.3
.

Then, we complete the proof as follows:

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|d_{I,z}(t)-d_{z}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|z_{I}(t)e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-z(t)e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-(z_{I}(t+\beta)-z(t+\beta))|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~2\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|z_{I}(t)e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-z(t)e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t+2\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|z_{I}(t+\beta)-z(t+\beta)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}^{2}T\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}+\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|z_{I}(t+\beta)-z(t+\beta)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}^{2}T\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}

where the first step follows from the definition of
d_{I,z}(t)
and
d_{z}(t)
, the second step follows from
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
, the third step follows from Eq. (
34
), the forth step follows from Lemma
F.3
.

∎

I.2
Ideal post-truncated local-test signal

It is still difficult to directly study the energy bound for
d_{z,I}(t)
. In this section, we further simplify the ideally filtered signal by removing the
H
filter and consider the untruncted ideally filtered signal
(x*I)(t)
. Then, in the local-test signal, we perform a post-truncation. More specifically, the untruncated ideally filtered signal and the
ideal post-truncated local-test signal
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle x_{I}(t):=

\displaystyle~(x*I)(t),

\displaystyle d_{I,x}(t):=

\displaystyle~x_{I}(t)\cdot H(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)\cdot H(t+\beta).

(35)

Intuitively,
d_{I,x}(t)
can be viewed as swapping the order of the
I
and
H
filters in
d_{I,z}(t)
.

The following lemma shows that
d_{I,z}(t)
and
d_{I,x}(t)
are actually the same!

Lemma I.3
.

Let
\delta_{1}
be defined as in Lemma
C.4
. Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
and
I=I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
be the corresponding ideal filter as in Eq. (
18
).

For any
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
, and
(\sigma,b)
such that Large Offset event does not happen, let
z_{I}(t)
and
d_{z,I}(t)
be defined as Eq. (
I.1
),
x_{I}(t)
and
d_{x,I}(t)
be defined as Eq. (
I.2
).

Then, we have

\displaystyle d_{I,z}(t)=d_{I,x}(t)~~~\forall t\in\mathbb{R}.

Proof.

We have that

\displaystyle d_{I,z}(t)=

\displaystyle~z_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-z_{I}(t+\beta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~x_{I}(t)\cdot H(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-z_{I}(t+\beta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~x_{I}(t)\cdot H(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)\cdot H(t+\beta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~d_{I,x}(t),

where the first step follows from the definition of
d_{I,z}(t)
, the second step follows from Lemma
F.1
, the third step follows from Lemma
F.1
, the last step follows from the definition of
d_{I,x}(t)
.
∎

The structure of
d_{I,x}(t)
makes it easy to study its magnitude at any “good point”:

Lemma I.4
.

Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
and
I=I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
be the corresponding ideal filter as in Eq. (
18
).
Let
U:=\{t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}~|~H(t)>1-\delta_{1}~\forall t\in[t_{0},t_{0}+\beta]\}
.

For any
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
, and
(\sigma,b)
such that Large Offset event does not happen, let
x_{I}(t),d_{x,I}(t)
be defined as Eq. (
I.2
). Then, we have

\displaystyle|d_{I,x}(t)|\lesssim\left|x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)\right|+\delta_{1}k\|x_{I}(t)\|_{T}~~~\forall t\in U.

Proof.

First, for any
t\in U
,

\displaystyle|x_{I}(t)\cdot H(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}|=

\displaystyle~|x_{I}(t)\cdot H(t)-x_{I}(t)|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~|x_{I}(t)|\cdot|1-H(t)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}|x_{I}(t)|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}k\|x_{I}(t)\|_{T}

(36)

where the first step follows from
|e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}|=1
, the second step is straight forward, the third step follows from
H(t)\leq 1
(Lemma
E.9
Property I, II) and
\forall t\in U,H(t)>1-\delta_{1}
, and the last step follows from Lemma
H.1
.

Second, for any
t\in U
,

\displaystyle|x_{I}(t+\beta)-x_{I}(t+\beta)\cdot H(t+\beta)|=

\displaystyle~|x_{I}(t+\beta)|\cdot|1-H(t+\beta)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}|x_{I}(t+\beta)|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}k\|x_{I}(t)\|_{T}

(37)

where the first step is straight forward, the second step follows from
H(t)\leq 1
(Lemma
E.9
Property I, II) and
\forall t\in U,H(t+\beta)>1-\delta_{1}
, the last step follows from Lemma
H.1
.

Combining them together, we have that for any
t\in U
,

\displaystyle|d_{I,x}(t)|=

\displaystyle~|x_{I}(t)\cdot H(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)\cdot H(t+\beta)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~|x_{I}(t)\cdot H(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}|+|x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)|

\displaystyle+|x_{I}(t+\beta)-x_{I}(t+\beta)\cdot H(t+\beta)|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~|x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)|+|x_{I}(t+\beta)-x_{I}(t+\beta)\cdot H(t+\beta)|+\delta_{1}k\|x_{I}(t)\|_{T}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~|x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)|+\delta_{1}k\|x_{I}(t)\|_{T}

where the first step follows from the definition of
d_{I,x}(t)
, the second step follows from triangle inequality, the third step follows Eq. (
36
), the forth step follows from Eq. (
37
).

∎

Furthermore, we can show that the ideal post-truncated local-test signal is close to the ideal local-test signal without truncation on most of “good points”.

Lemma I.5
.

Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
and
I=I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
be the corresponding ideal filter as in Eq. (
18
).
Let
U:=\{t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}~|~H(t)>1-\delta_{1},\forall t\in[t_{0},t_{0}+\beta]\}
. Let
D_{U}(t):=\mathrm{Uniform}(U)
and
D_{U+\beta}(t):=\mathrm{Uniform}(U+\beta)
.

For any
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
, and
(\sigma,b)
such that Large Offset event does not happen, let
x_{I}(t),d_{x,I}(t)
be defined as Eq. (
I.2
). Then, we have

\displaystyle\|d_{I,x}(t)-(x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta))\|_{D_{U}}\lesssim\delta_{1}\|x_{I}(t)\|_{T}.

Proof.

First,

\displaystyle\|x_{I}(t)\cdot H(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}\|_{D_{U}}=

\displaystyle~\|x_{I}(t)\cdot H(t)-x_{I}(t)\|_{D_{U}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~{\max}_{t\in U}\{|1-H(t)|\}\cdot\|x_{I}(t)\|_{D_{U}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}\cdot\|x_{I}(t)\|_{D_{U}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{T}{|U|}}\|x_{I}(t)\|_{T}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}\cdot\|x_{I}(t)\|_{T}

(38)

where the first step follows from
|e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}|=1
, the second step is straight forward, the third step follows from
H(t)\leq 1
(Lemma
E.9
Property I, II) and
\forall t\in U,H(t)>1-\delta_{1}
, the forth step follows from the definition of the norm

\displaystyle\|x(t)\|^{2}_{D_{U}}=\frac{1}{|U|}\int_{U}|x(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{1}{|U|}\int_{[0,T]}|x(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t=\frac{T}{|U|}\|x(t)\|^{2}_{T},

and the last step follows from Lemma
H.1
.

Second,

\displaystyle\|x_{I}(t+\beta)-x_{I}(t+\beta)\cdot H(t+\beta)\|_{D_{U}}\leq

\displaystyle~{\max}_{t\in U}\{|1-H(t+\beta)|\}\cdot\|x_{I}(t+\beta)\|_{D_{U}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}\cdot\|x_{I}(t)\|_{D_{U+\beta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}\cdot\frac{1}{|U+\beta|}\|x_{I}(t)\|_{D_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}\cdot\|x_{I}(t)\|_{D_{1}}

(39)

where the first step is straight forward, the second step follows from
H(t)\leq 1
(Lemma
E.9
Property I, II) and
\forall t\in U,H(t+\beta)>1-\delta_{1}
, the third step follows from the definition of the norm, the forth step follows from
|U+\beta|=|U|\gtrsim 1
.

Then, we have that,

\displaystyle~\|d_{I,x}(t)-(x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta))\|_{D_{U}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\|x_{I}(t)\cdot H(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)\cdot H(t+\beta)-(x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta))\|_{D_{U}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\|x_{I}(t)\cdot H(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}\|_{D_{U}}+\|x_{I}(t+\beta)\cdot H(t+\beta)-x_{I}(t+\beta)\|_{D_{U}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}\cdot\|x_{I}(t)\|_{D_{1}}+\|x_{I}(t+\beta)\cdot H(t+\beta)-x_{I}(t+\beta)\|_{D_{U}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}\cdot\|x_{I}(t)\|_{D_{1}}

where the first step follows from the definition of
d_{I,x}(t)
, the second step follows from triangle inequality, the third step follows from Eq. (
38
), the forth step follows from Eq. (
39
).

∎

I.3
Energy bound for local-test signals

In this section, we prove the following lemma, which gives an energy bound for local-test signals.

Lemma I.6

(Energy bound for local-test signals)
.

Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
.
Let
U
,
D_{U}
be defined as in Lemma
I.5
.

For any
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
, and
(\sigma,b)
such that Large Offset event does not happen, let
z(t)=(x\cdot H)*G_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}(t)
and
d_{z}(t)
be defined as Eq. (
32
). Then, we have

\displaystyle{|d_{z}(t)|^{2}}\lesssim{\min}\Big\{\frac{k}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot{\|d_{z}(t)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}}+\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}~~~\forall t\in U.

Proof.

Let
I=I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
be the corresponding ideal filter as in Eq. (
18
).
Let
S:=\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})
be the support set of
\widehat{x}*\widehat{H}
.
We have
|S|\leq\Delta
.

Let
z_{I}(t)
,
d_{z,I}(t)
be defined as in Lemma
I.1
and
x_{I}(t)
,
d_{I,x}(t)
be defined as in Lemma
I.3
.

Before proving the energy bound for
|d_{z}(t)|
, we first consider the signal
x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)
.
By Fourier transformation, we know that its Fourier coefficient of a frequency
f
is:

\displaystyle\widehat{x}_{I}(f)e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-\widehat{x}_{I}(f)e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f\beta}=\widehat{x}(f)\cdot\widehat{I}(f)e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-\widehat{x}(f)\cdot\widehat{I}(f)e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f\beta}

Thus,
x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)
is at most
k
-Fourier-sparse.

Let
[L,R]:=U
. By Fourier-sparse signals’ energy bound (Theorem
B.2
and Theorem
B.1
), we have

\displaystyle|x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)|^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k}{\min\{R-t,t-L\}},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot\|x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\min}\{\frac{k}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\}\cdot\|x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}

(40)

where the first step follows from applying Theorem
B.2
with
x(t)=x(Tt/2+T/2)
and applying Theorem
B.1
with
T=|U|,x(t)=x(t+L)
, the second step follows from
[-1+0.5/k,1-0.5/k]\subseteq[L,R]
, which implies that
k(\min\{R-t,t-L\})^{-1}\lesssim k(1-|2t/T-1|)^{-1}
for any
|t|\in[L+1/k,R-1/k]
. Moreover, for any
|t|\in[L,L+1/k]\cup[R-1/k,R]
,
k^{2}\lesssim k(1-|2t/T-1|)^{-1}
.

The RHS can be upper bounded by:

\displaystyle\|x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle~2\|d_{I,x}(t)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}+2\|d_{I,x}(t)-(x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta))\|_{D_{U}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\|d_{I,x}(t)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}+\delta_{1}^{2}\|x_{I}(t)\|^{2}_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\|d_{I,z}(t)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}+\delta_{1}^{2}\|x_{I}(t)\|^{2}_{T}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\|d_{z}(t)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}+\|d_{I,z}(t)-d_{z}(t)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}+\delta_{1}^{2}\|x_{I}(t)\|^{2}_{T}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\|d_{z}(t)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}+\|d_{I,z}(t)-d_{z}(t)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}+\delta_{1}^{2}\|x(t)\|^{2}_{T}

(41)

where the first step follows from
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
, the second step follows from Lemma
I.5
, the third step follows from Lemma
I.3
, the forth step follows from
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
, the last step follows from Claim
I.7
.
For the second term, we have that

\displaystyle~\|d_{I,z}(t)-d_{z}(t)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{|U|}\int_{U}|d_{I,z}(t)-d_{z}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{|U|}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|d_{I,z}(t)-d_{z}(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{|U|}\delta_{1}^{2}\|x(t)\|^{2}_{T}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}^{2}\|x(t)\|^{2}_{T}

where the first step follows from the definition of the norm, second step is straight forward, the third step follows from Lemma
I.2
with appropriate scaling, the forth step follows from
|U|\gtrsim 1
. Hence,

\displaystyle\|x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}\lesssim\|d_{z}(t)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}+\delta_{1}^{2}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}.

(42)

Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle|d_{I,z}(t)|^{2}=

\displaystyle~|d_{I,x}(t)|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~(|x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)|+\delta_{1}k\|x_{I}(t)\|_{T})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~|x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)|^{2}+\delta_{1}^{2}k^{2}\|x_{I}(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~|x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)|^{2}+\delta_{1}\|x_{I}(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\min}\{\frac{k}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\}\cdot\|x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}+\delta_{1}\|x_{I}(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\min}\{\frac{k}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\}\cdot\|x_{I}(t)\cdot e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-x_{I}(t+\beta)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}+\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\min}\{\frac{k}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\}\cdot(\|d_{z}(t)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}+\delta_{1}^{2}\|x(t)\|^{2}_{T})+\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\min}\{\frac{k}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\}\cdot\|d_{z}(t)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}+\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2},

(43)

where the first step follows from Lemma
I.3
, the second step follows from Lemma
I.4
, the third step follows from
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
, the forth step follows from
\delta_{1}k^{2}\leq 1
, the fifth step follows from Eq. (
40
), the six step follows from Claim
I.7
, the seventh step follows from Eq. (
42
), the last step follows from
\delta_{1}k^{2}\lesssim 1
.

Finally, we have

\displaystyle|d_{z}(t)|^{2}\leq

\displaystyle~2|d_{z}(t)-d_{I,z}(t)|^{2}+2|d_{I,z}(t)|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~2\delta_{1}^{2}T|S|\cdot\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}+2|d_{I,z}(t)|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~2\delta_{1}\cdot\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}+2|d_{I,z}(t)|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\delta_{1}\cdot\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}+{\min}\{\frac{k}{1-|2t/T-1|},k^{2}\}\cdot\|d_{z}(t)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}

where the first step follows from
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
, the second step follows from Lemma
I.1
, the third step follows from
\delta_{1}T|S|\leq 1
, the forth step follows from Eq. (
43
).

The lemma is then proved.

∎

Claim I.7

(Energy Reduction by Ideal Filter)
.

Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
and
I=I_{\sigma,b}^{(j)}
be the corresponding ideal filter as in Eq. (
18
).

For any
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
, for any
(\sigma,b)
such that Large Offset event does not happen, then we have

\displaystyle\|(x*I)(t)\|_{T}\lesssim\|x(t)\|_{T}

Proof.

Let
S=\mathrm{supp}(\widehat{x}*\widehat{H})
.
We have that

\displaystyle T\|(x*I)(t)\|_{T}\lesssim

\displaystyle~T\|(x*I)(t)\cdot H(t)\|_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\int_{0}^{T}|(x*I)(t)\cdot H(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|(x*I)(t)\cdot H(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|(\widehat{x}\cdot\widehat{I})(f)*\widehat{H}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\int_{S}|(\widehat{x}\cdot\widehat{I})(f)*\widehat{H}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\int_{S}|\widehat{x}(f)*\widehat{H}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|\widehat{x}(f)*\widehat{H}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|x\cdot H(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~\int_{0}^{T}|x(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~T\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}

where the first step follows from Lemma
E.9
Property V, the second step follows from the definition of the norm, the third step is straight forward, the forth step follows from Parseval’s theorem, the fifth and sixth steps follow from Large Offset event not happening, the seventh step is straight forward, the eighth step follows from Parseval’s theorem, the ninth step follows from Lemma
E.9
Property IV and VI, the last step follows from the definition of the norm.

∎

Figure 11:
An illustration of the energy reduction by ideal filter.
I_{\sigma,b}
is the ideal filter and
x^{*}(t)
is a Fourier sparse signal. The energy of
x^{*}(t)
in duration
[0,T]
is reduced by applying the ideal filter, i.e.,
\|x^{*}*I_{\sigma,b}(t)\|_{T}\lesssim\|x^{*}(t)\|_{T}
.

Appendix J
Empirical Energy Estimation

The goal of this section is to show how to estimate a signal’s energy using a few samples. We start with a general sampling and reweighing method (see Section
J.1
). Then, combining with the energy bounds derived in previous section, we obtain sample-efficient energy estimation methods for Fourier-sparse signals and filtered signals (see Section
J.2
).
We further extend our methods to estimate the energy of filtered signals and local-test signals within a
sub-interval
in the time duration (see Section
J.3
). Finally, we prove several technical lemmas (see Section
J.4
).

Throughout this section, for the convenience, we use a slightly different notation for the
T
-norm:

\displaystyle\|z\|_{T}^{2}:=\frac{1}{2T}\int_{-T}^{T}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t.

This results of using this
T
-norm is equivalent with the result of the norm taking on
[0,T]
, since we can always re-scaling the signal and transform the result into the new
T
-norm result.

J.1
Sampling and reweighing

In this section, we provide a generic sample-efficient method for estimating the energy of any function using discrete samples with proper weights.

Lemma J.1
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
D
be a probability distribution such that
\int_{-T}^{T}D(t)\mathrm{d}t=1
.
For any
\varepsilon,\rho\in(0,1)
and function
z:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
, let
S_{D}=\{t_{1},\cdots,t_{s}\}
be a set of i.i.d. samples from
D
of size

\displaystyle s\geq\Big({\max}_{t\in[-T,T]}~\frac{|z(t)|^{2}}{D(t)}\Big)\cdot O\Big(\frac{\log(1/\rho)}{\varepsilon^{2}T\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}}\Big).

Let the weight vector
w\in\mathbb{R}^{s}
be defined by
w_{i}:=1/(2TsD(t_{i}))
for
i\in[s]
.

Then with probability at least
1-\rho
, we have

(1-\varepsilon)\|z(t)\|^{2}_{T}\leq\|z(t)\|^{2}_{S_{D},w}\leq(1+\varepsilon)\|z(t)\|^{2}_{T},

where
\|z\|_{T}^{2}:=\frac{1}{2T}\int_{-T}^{T}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t
.

Proof.

Let
M:={\max}_{t\in[-T,T]}\frac{|z(t)|^{2}}{D(t)}
. Let
z_{D}(t):=\frac{1}{M}\frac{|z(t)|^{2}}{D(t)}
. By applying Chernoff bound (Lemma
A.1
) for the random variables
z_{D}(t_{1}),\dots,z_{D}(t_{s})
, we get that,

\displaystyle\Pr_{t_{i}\sim D}\Big[\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{s}z_{D}(t_{i})-\mu\Big|\leq\varepsilon\mu\Big]\geq 1-2\exp(-\varepsilon^{2}\mu/3),

(44)

where
\mu:=\sum_{i=1}^{s}\E_{t_{i}\sim D}[z_{D}(t_{i})]=s\cdot\E_{t\sim D}[z_{D}(t)]
.

We first consider the expectation:

\displaystyle\E_{t\sim D}[z_{D}(t)]=

\displaystyle~\int_{-T}^{T}D(t)\cdot\frac{1}{M}\frac{|z(t)|^{2}}{D(t)}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\frac{1}{M}\int_{-T}^{T}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\frac{2T}{M}\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}

where the first step follows from the definition of expectation, the second step is straightforward, the third step follows from the definition of the norm. Thus,

\displaystyle\mu=s\cdot\E_{t\sim D}[z_{D}(t)]=\frac{2Ts}{M}\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}.

(45)

Then, we consider the sum of samples:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{s}z_{D}(t_{i})=

\displaystyle~\sum_{i=1}^{s}\frac{1}{M}\frac{|z(t_{i})|^{2}}{D(t_{i})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{i=1}^{s}\frac{2w_{i}Ts}{M}{|z(t_{i})|^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\frac{2Ts}{M}\|z(t)\|^{2}_{S_{D},w}

(46)

where the first step follows from the definition of
z_{D}
, the second step follows from the definition of
w_{i}
, the last step follows from the definition of the norm.

Putting Eqs. (
44
) - (
46
) together, we get that with probability at least
1-2\exp(-\varepsilon^{2}\mu/3)
,

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{2Ts}{M}\|z(t)\|_{S_{D},w}^{2}-\frac{2Ts}{M}\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}\Big|\leq\varepsilon\cdot\frac{2Ts}{M}\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2},

which can be simplified at follows:

\displaystyle|\|z(t)\|_{S_{D},w}^{2}-\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}|\leq\varepsilon\cdot\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}.

Finally, we need the success probability to be at least
1-\rho
, which requires that:

\displaystyle 1-2\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{3}\frac{2Ts}{M}\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}\Big)=

\displaystyle~1-2\exp\Big(-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{3}\frac{2Ts}{\cdot{\max}_{t\in[-T,T]}\{{|z(t)|^{2}}/{D(t)}\}}\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~1-\rho.

Hence, we need the sample complexity
s
to be at least

\displaystyle s\geq\Big({\max}_{t\in[-T,T]}~\frac{|z(t)|^{2}}{D(t)}\Big)\cdot O\Big(\frac{\log(1/\rho)}{\varepsilon^{2}T\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}}\Big).

∎

J.2
Energy estimation for Fourier-sparse signals and filtered signals

The goal of this section is to apply Lemma
J.1
for Fourier-sparse signals and filtered signals.

The following lemma defines the sampling distribution:

Lemma J.2
.

For
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, define a probability distribution
D
as follows:

\displaystyle D(t):=\begin{cases}{c}\cdot(1-|t/T|)^{-1}T^{-1},&\text{ for }|t|\leq T(1-{1}/k)\\
c\cdot kT^{-1},&\text{ for }|t|\in[T(1-{1}/k),T]\end{cases}

(47)

where
c=\Theta(\log(k)^{-1})
is a normalization factor such that
\int_{-T}^{T}D(t)\mathrm{d}t=1
. Then,
D
is well-defined.

Proof.

We justify that
D
can be normalized with
c=\Theta(\log(k)^{-1}))
.
By the condition
\int_{-T}^{T}D(t)\mathrm{d}t=1
, we have

\displaystyle 2\int_{0}^{T(1-{1}/k)}\frac{c}{(1-|t/T|)T}\mathrm{d}t+2\int_{T(1-{1}/k)}^{T}c\cdot\frac{k}{T}\mathrm{d}t=1,

which implies that

\displaystyle c^{-1}=

\displaystyle~2\int_{0}^{T(1-{1}/k)}\frac{1}{(1-|t/T|)T}\mathrm{d}t+2\int_{T(1-{1}/k)}^{T}\frac{k}{T}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\eqsim

\displaystyle~\log(k)+1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\Theta(\log(k)).

Thus, we get that
c=\Theta(\log(k)^{-1})
.

∎

The following lemma gives the sampling complexity for estimating the energy of a Fourier-sparse signal. The main idea is to apply the energy bounds in Section
B
.

Lemma J.3

(Energy estimation for Fourier-sparse signals)
.

Let
D
be the probability distribution defined as Eq. (
47
).
Let
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
.
For any
\varepsilon,\rho\in(0,1)
, let
S_{D}=\{t_{1},\cdots,t_{s}\}
be a set of i.i.d. samples from
D(t)
of size
s\geq O(\varepsilon^{-2}k\log(k)\log(1/\rho))
. Let the weight vector
w\in\mathbb{R}^{s}
be defined by
w_{i}:=1/(2TsD(t_{i}))
for
i\in[s]
.

Then with probability at least
1-\rho
, we have

(1-\varepsilon)\|x(t)\|^{2}_{T}\leq\|x(t)\|^{2}_{S_{D},w}\leq(1+\varepsilon)\|x(t)\|^{2}_{T}.

Proof.

By applying Lemma
J.1
, we have that the desired result satisfy when

\displaystyle s\geq\Big({\max}_{t\in[-T,T]}~\frac{|x(t)|^{2}}{D(t)}\Big)\cdot O\Big(\frac{\log(1/\rho)}{\varepsilon^{2}T\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}}\Big).

By Fourier-sparse signals’ energy bound (Theorem
B.1
and Theorem
B.2
with
x(t)=x(T\cdot t)
), we have that

\displaystyle{|x(t)|^{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k}{1-|t/T|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot{\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}}~~~\forall t\in[-T,T].

(48)

Thus,

\displaystyle~{\max}_{t\in[-T,T]}\frac{|x(t)|^{2}}{D(t)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\max}_{t\in[-T,T]}~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k}{1-|t/T|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot\frac{\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}}{D(t)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\max}_{t\in[-T,T]}~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k}{1-|t/T|}\frac{T(1-|t/T|)}{c},k^{2}\frac{T}{ck}\Big\}\cdot\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~kT\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}/c

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle~k\log(k)T\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2},

(49)

where the first step follows from Eq. (
48
), the second step follows from the definition of
D(t)
, the third step is straight forward, the forth step follows from
c=\Theta(\log(k)^{-1})
.

Hence, we get that

\displaystyle s\geq O(k\log(k)T\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2})\cdot O\Big(\frac{\log(1/\rho)}{\varepsilon^{2}T\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}}\Big)=O(\varepsilon^{-2}k\log(k)\log(1/\rho)).

The lemma is then proved.

∎

Using the energy bound for filtered signals, we immediately get the following lemma.

Lemma J.4

(Energy estimation for filtered signals)
.

Let
D
be the probability distribution defined as Eq. (
47
).
Let
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
.
Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
. Let
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
.
Let
j\in[B]
satisfying that there exists an
f^{*}
with
h_{\sigma,b}(f^{*})=j
such that:

\displaystyle\int_{f^{*}-{\Delta_{h}}}^{f^{*}+{\Delta_{h}}}|\widehat{x\cdot H}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f\geq T\mathcal{N}^{2}/k,

where
\mathcal{N}^{2}\geq\delta\|x\|_{T}^{2}
.
Let
z(t):=(x\cdot H)*{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)
be the filtered signal.

For any
\varepsilon,\rho\in(0,1)
, let
S_{D}=\{t_{1},\cdots,t_{s}\}
be a set of i.i.d. samples from
D(t)
of size
s\geq O(\varepsilon^{-2}k\log(k)\log(1/\rho))
. Let the weight vector
w\in\mathbb{R}^{s}
be defined by
w_{i}:=1/(2TsD(t_{i}))
for
i\in[s]
.

Then when Large Offset event not happens, with probability at least
1-\rho
, we have

(1-\varepsilon)\|z(t)\|^{2}_{T}\leq\|z(t)\|^{2}_{S_{D},w}\leq(1+\varepsilon)\|z(t)\|^{2}_{T}.

Proof.

By applying Lemma
J.1
, we have that the desired result requires that

\displaystyle s\geq\Big({\max}_{t\in[-T,T]}~\frac{|z(t)|^{2}}{D(t)}\Big)\cdot O\Big(\frac{\log(1/\rho)}{\varepsilon^{2}T\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}}\Big).

By the filtered signals’ energy bound (Corollary
H.4
), we have that

\displaystyle{|z(t)|^{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k\cdot H(t)+\delta}{1-|t/T|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot{\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k}{1-|t/T|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot{\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}}.

(50)

where the second step follows from
H(t)\lesssim 1
(Lemma
E.9
Property I, II).
Then, we get that

\displaystyle~{\max}_{t\in[-T,T]}~\frac{|z(t)|^{2}}{D(t)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\max}_{t\in[-T,T]}~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k}{1-|t/T|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot{\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\max}_{t\in[-T,T]}~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k}{1-|t/T|}\frac{T(1-|t/T|)}{c},k^{2}\frac{T}{ck}\Big\}\cdot\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~kT\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}/c

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle~k\log(k)T\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2},

(51)

where the first step follows from Eq. (
50
), the second step follows from the definition of
D(t)
, the third step is straight forward, the forth step follows from
c=\Theta(\log(k)^{-1})
.

As a result,

\displaystyle s\geq O(k\log(k)T\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2})\cdot O\Big(\frac{\log(1/\rho)}{\varepsilon^{2}T\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}}\Big)=O(\varepsilon^{-2}k\log(k)\log(1/\rho)).

The lemma is then proved.

∎

J.3
Partial energy estimation for filtered signals and local-test signals

In this section, we consider a variant version of energy estimation problem, which we are given a sub-interval
U\subseteq[-T,T]
and we only want to estimate the energy within this interval.

The following lemma gives the sampling distribution with respect to
U
.

Lemma J.5
.

Let
U=[L,R]
such that
[-T(1-{1}/k),T(1-{1}/k)]\subseteq U\subseteq[-T,T]
. For
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, define a probability distribution
D_{U}
as follows:

\displaystyle D_{U}(t):=\begin{cases}{c}\cdot(1-|t/T|)^{-1}T^{-1},&\text{ for }|t|\leq T(1-{1}/k)\wedge t\in U\\
c\cdot kT^{-1},&\text{ for }|t|\in[T(1-{1}/k),T]\wedge t\in U\end{cases}

(52)

where
c=\Theta(\log(k)^{-1})
is a normalization factor such that
\int_{-T}^{T}D_{U}(t)\mathrm{d}t=1
. Then,
D_{U}
is well-defined.

Proof.

We compute the normalization factor of
D_{U}
in below. The condition that
\int_{-T}^{T}D_{U}(t)\mathrm{d}t=1
requires that

\displaystyle 2\int_{0}^{T(1-{1}/k)}\frac{c}{(1-|t/T|)T}\mathrm{d}t+\int_{T(1-{1}/k)}^{R}c\cdot\frac{k}{T}\mathrm{d}t+\int_{L}^{-T(1-{1}/k)}c\cdot\frac{k}{T}\mathrm{d}t=1,

which implies that

\displaystyle c^{-1}=

\displaystyle~2\int_{0}^{T(1-{1}/k)}\frac{1}{(1-|t/T|)T}\mathrm{d}t+\int_{T(1-{1}/k)}^{R}\frac{k}{T}\mathrm{d}t+\int_{L}^{-T(1-{1}/k)}\frac{k}{T}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\eqsim

\displaystyle~\log(k)+1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\Theta(\log(k)).

where the second step follows from
R\leq T
and
L\geq-T
.

Thus, we get that
c=\Theta(\log(k)^{-1})
.
∎

Similar to Lemma
J.4
, we have a sample-efficient approach for estimating the partial energy of a filtered signal.

Lemma J.6

(Partial energy estimation for filtered signals)
.

Let
U=[L,R]
be such that
[-T(1-{1}/k),T(1-{1}/k)]\subseteq U
. For
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, let
D_{U}
be the probability distribution defined as Eq. (
52
).

Let
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
.
Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
with
(\sigma,b)
such that Large Offset event does not happen.
For any
j\in[B]
, suppose there exists an
f^{*}
with
j=h_{\sigma,b}(f^{*})
satisfying:

\displaystyle\int_{f^{*}-{\Delta_{h}}}^{f^{*}+{\Delta_{h}}}|\widehat{x\cdot H}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f\geq T\mathcal{N}^{2}/k,

where
\mathcal{N}^{2}\geq\delta\|x\|_{T}^{2}
.
Let
z(t)=(x\cdot H)*{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)
be the filtered signal.

For any
\varepsilon,\rho\in(0,1)
, let
S_{D_{U}}=\{t_{1},\cdots,t_{s}\}
be a set of i.i.d. samples from
D_{U}
of size
s\geq O(\varepsilon^{-2}k\log(k)\log(1/\rho))
. Let the weight vector
w\in\mathbb{R}^{s}
be defined by
w_{i}:=2/(TsD_{U}(t_{i}))
for
i\in[s]
.

Then when Large Offset event not happens, with probability at least
1-\rho
, we have

(1-\varepsilon)\|z\|^{2}_{U}\leq\|z\|^{2}_{S_{D_{U}},w}\leq(1+\varepsilon)\|z\|^{2}_{U},

where
\|z\|_{U}^{2}:=\frac{1}{R-L}\cdot\int_{L}^{R}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t
.

Proof.

By applying Lemma
J.1
, we have that the desired result requires that

\displaystyle s\geq\Big({\max}_{t\in U}~\frac{|z(t)|^{2}}{D_{U}(t)}\Big)\cdot O\Big(\frac{\log(1/\rho)}{\varepsilon^{2}T\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}}\Big).

The first term can be upper bounded as follows:

\displaystyle{\max}_{t\in U}~\frac{|z(t)|^{2}}{D_{U}(t)}\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\max}_{t\in U}~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k}{1-|t/T|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot\frac{\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}}{D_{U}(t)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\max}_{t\in U}~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k}{1-|t/T|}\frac{T(1-|t/T|)}{c},k^{2}\frac{T}{ck}\Big\}\cdot\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~kT\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}/c

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~k\log(k)T\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~k\log(k)\frac{R-L}{2T-k^{2}(2T+L-R)}\cdot T\|z(t)\|_{U}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~k\log(k)\cdot T\|z(t)\|_{U}^{2},

(53)

where the first step follows from Eq. (
50
), the second step follows from the definition of
D_{U}(t)
, the third step is straight forward, the forth step follows from
c=\Theta(\log(k)^{-1})
, the fifth step follows from Lemma
J.9
, the sixth step follows from
{R-L}\leq{2T-k^{2}(2T+L-R)}
.

Therefore, the sample complexity
s
should be at least:

\displaystyle s\geq O(k\log(k)\cdot T\|z(t)\|_{U}^{2})\cdot O\Big(\frac{\log(1/\rho)}{\varepsilon^{2}T\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}}\Big)=O(\varepsilon^{-2}k\log(k)\log(1/\rho)).

The proof of the lemma is then completed.

∎

Recall that in Section
I
, we study the local-test signal
d_{z}(t)=z(t)e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-z(t+\beta)
. The following lemma gives a way to estimate the partial energy of a local-test signal. It can be proved by the same strategy with the energy bound in Lemma
I.6
.

Lemma J.7

(Partial energy estimation for local-test signals)
.

Let
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
.
Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
with
(\sigma,b)
such that Large Offset event does not happen.
Let
z(t)=(x\cdot H)*{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)
be the filtered signal.
Let
U:=\{t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}~|~H(t)>1-\delta_{1},\forall t\in[t_{0},t_{0}+\beta]\}
. Let
D_{U}
be the probability distribution defined as Eq. (
52
).
Let
D_{H}(t):=\mathrm{Uniform}(\{t\in\mathbb{R}~|~H(t)>1-\delta_{1}\})
.

For any
\varepsilon,\rho\in(0,1)
, let
S_{D_{U}}=\{t_{1},\cdots,t_{s}\}
be a set of i.i.d. samples from
D_{U}
of size
s\geq O(k\log(k)\log(1/\rho))
. Let the weight vector
w\in\mathbb{R}^{s}
be defined by
w_{i}:=2/(TsD_{U}(t_{i}))
for
i\in[s]
.

Let
d_{z}(t)=z(t)e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}f_{0}\beta}-z(t+\beta)
be the local-test signal. Then, with probability at least
1-\rho
, we have

\displaystyle\|d_{z}(t)\|^{2}_{S_{D_{U}},w}\leq 2\|d_{z}(t)\|^{2}_{U}+\sqrt{\delta_{1}}\|x(t)\|_{T}\cdot\|d_{z}(t)\|_{U}.

Proof.

By Lemma
J.1
, we have that when

\displaystyle s\geq\Big({\max}_{t\in U}~\frac{|d_{z}(t)|^{2}}{D_{U}(t)}\Big)\cdot O\Big(\frac{\log(1/\rho)}{\xi^{2}|U|\cdot\|d_{z}(t)\|_{U}^{2}}\Big),

the following result holds with probability at least
1-\rho
,

\displaystyle\|d_{z}(t)\|^{2}_{S_{D_{U}},w}\in(1\pm\xi)\|d_{z}(t)\|_{U}^{2},

(54)

where
\xi
is a parameter to be chosen later.

By the energy bound for local-test signals (Lemma
I.6
), we have that for any
t\in U
,

\displaystyle{|d_{z}(t)|^{2}}\lesssim{\min}\Big\{\frac{k}{1-|t/T|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot{\|d_{z}(t)\|_{U}^{2}}+\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}.

(55)

Then, we get that

\displaystyle~{\max}_{t\in U}~\frac{|d_{z}(t)|^{2}}{D_{U}(t)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\max}_{t\in U}~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k}{1-|t/T|},k^{2}\Big\}\cdot\frac{\|d_{z}(t)\|_{U}^{2}+\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}}{D_{U}(t)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle~{\max}_{t\in U}~{\min}\Big\{\frac{k}{1-|t/T|}\frac{T(1-|t/T|)}{c},k^{2}\frac{T}{ck}\Big\}\cdot(\|d_{z}(t)\|_{U}^{2}+\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~kTc^{-1}\cdot(\|d_{z}(t)\|_{U}^{2}+\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle~k\log(k)T\cdot(\|d_{z}(t)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}+\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{D_{1}}^{2}),

(56)

where the first step follows from Eq. (
55
), the second step follows from the definition of
D_{U}(t)
, the third step is straight forward, the forth step follows from
c=\Theta(\log(k)^{-1})
.

As a result, the sample complexity is

\displaystyle s\geq

\displaystyle~k\log(k)T\cdot(\|d_{z}(t)\|_{D_{U}}^{2}+\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{D_{1}}^{2})\cdot O\Big(\frac{\log(1/\rho)}{\xi^{2}|U|\cdot\|d_{z}(t)\|_{U}^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle~\xi^{-2}\cdot k\log(k)\cdot(1+\frac{\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}}{\|d_{z}(t)\|_{U}^{2}})\cdot\log(1/\rho)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~k\log(k)\cdot\log(1/\rho),

where the first step follows from Eq. (
56
), the second step follows from
|U|\gtrsim T
, the third step follows by taking
\xi
to be such that

\displaystyle\xi^{-2}(1+\frac{\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}}{\|d_{z}(t)\|_{U}^{2}})\simeq 1.

It remains to bound the estimation error. We have that

\displaystyle\|d_{z}(t)\|^{2}_{S_{D},w}\leq

\displaystyle~(1+\xi)\|d_{z}(t)\|_{U}^{2}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle~\Big(1+\sqrt{1+\frac{\delta_{1}\|x(t)\|_{D_{1}}^{2}}{\|d_{z}(t)\|_{U}^{2}}}\Big)\|d_{z}(t)\|_{U}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~\Big(2+{\frac{\sqrt{\delta_{1}}\|x(t)\|_{T}}{\|d_{z}(t)\|_{U}}}\Big)\|d_{z}(t)\|_{U}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle~2\|d_{z}(t)\|_{U}^{2}+{{\sqrt{\delta_{1}}\|x(t)\|_{T}}\cdot{\|d_{z}(t)\|_{U}}}

where the first step follows from Eq. (
54
), the second step follows from the setting of
\varepsilon
, the third step follows from
\sqrt{a+b}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}
, the forth step is straight forward.

The lemma is then proved.
∎

J.4
Technical lemmas

We prove two technical lemmas in this section.

The following lemma bounds the energy of a Fourier-sparse signal within time duration
[L,R]\subseteq[-T,T]
by its total energy.

Lemma J.8

(Partial energy of Fourier-sparse signal)
.

Given
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+},F\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.
For any
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
,
[L,R]\subseteq[-T(1-O(\frac{1}{k^{2}})),T(1-O(\frac{1}{k^{2}}))]
, we have that,

\displaystyle\frac{2T-k^{2}(2T+L-R)}{R-L}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{R-L}\int_{L}^{R}|x(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{2T}{R-L}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}.

Proof.

For the upper bound, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{R-L}\int_{L}^{R}|x(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{1}{R-L}\int_{-T}^{T}|x(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{2T}{R-L}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2},

where the first step is straight forward, the second step follows from the definition of the norm.

For the lower bound, we have that

\displaystyle\int_{L}^{R}|x(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t=

\displaystyle~\int_{-T}^{T}|x(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t-\int_{-T}^{L}|x(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t-\int_{R}^{T}|x(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~2T\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}-(L+T)\cdot{\max}_{t\in[-T,L]}|x(t)|^{2}-(T-R)\cdot{\max}_{t\in[R,T]}|x(t)|^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle~2T\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}-(L+T)\cdot k^{2}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}-(T-R)\cdot k^{2}\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(2T-k^{2}(2T+L-R))\|x(t)\|_{T}^{2},

where the first step is straight forward, the second step follows from the definition of the norm, the third step follows from Theorem
B.1
, the forth step is straight forward.

∎

By replacing the energy bound for Fourier-sparse signals with the energy bound for filtered signals (Corollary
H.4
), we obtain the following lemma:

Lemma J.9

(Partial energy of filtered signal)
.

Given
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
F\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Let
x\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
.
Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
with
(\sigma,b)
such that Large Offset event does not happen.

For any
j\in[B]
, suppose there exists an
f^{*}
with
j=h_{\sigma,b}(f^{*})
satisfying:

\displaystyle\int_{f^{*}-{\Delta_{h}}}^{f^{*}+{\Delta_{h}}}|\widehat{x\cdot H}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f\geq T\mathcal{N}^{2}/k,

where
\mathcal{N}^{2}\geq\delta\|x\|_{T}^{2}
.
Then, for
z(t)=(x\cdot H)*{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)
, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{2T-k^{2}(2T+L-R)}{R-L}\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{R-L}\int_{L}^{R}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{2T}{R-L}\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}.

Proof.

For the upper bound, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{R-L}\int_{L}^{R}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{1}{R-L}\int_{-T}^{T}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{2T}{R-L}\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2},

where the first step is straight forward, the second step follows from the definition of the norm.

For the lower bound, we have that

\displaystyle\int_{L}^{R}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t=

\displaystyle~\int_{-T}^{T}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t-\int_{-T}^{L}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t-\int_{R}^{T}|z(t)|^{2}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle~T\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}-(L+T)\cdot{\max}_{t\in[0,L]}|z(t)|^{2}-(T-R)\cdot{\max}_{t\in[R,T]}|z(t)|^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle~T\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}-(L+T)\cdot k^{2}\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}-(T-R)k^{2}\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~(2T-k^{2}(2T+L-R))\|z(t)\|_{T}^{2}

where the first step is straight forward, the second step follows from the definition of the norm, the third step follows from Corollary
H.4
, the forth step is straight forward.

∎

Appendix K
Generate Significant Samples

In this section, we show our significant sample generation procedure for noisy signals.
Recall that we use
x^{*}(t)
to denote the ground-truth
k
-Fourier-sparse signal and
x(t)=x^{*}(t)+g(t)
to denote the observation signal. We first generalize the energy estimation method in previous section to the noisy signals (see Section
K.1
). Then, we give our significant sample generation algorithm for a single bin (see Section
K.2
). Next, we show how to adapt our significant sample generation algorithm for multiple bins (see Section
K.3
). In addition, we provide some technical claims (see Section
K.4
).

K.1
Energy estimation for noisy signals

In this section, we generalize our methods in Section
J
to estimate the (partial) energy of the true observing signals, which contains some noise.

In the following lemma, we show that the energy of the filtered signal
z(t)
can be estimated with a few samples, assuming it contains a small fraction of noise.

Lemma K.1
.

Let
x^{*}\in{\cal F}_{k,F}
be the ground-truth signal and
x(t)=x^{*}(t)+g(t)
be the noisy observation signal.
Let
H
be defined as in Definition
E.5
,
{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}
be defined as in Definition
C.2
with
(\sigma,b)
such that Large Offset event does not happen.
For any
j\in[B]
, suppose there exists an
f_{0}

with
j=h_{\sigma,b}(f_{0})
satisfying:

\displaystyle\int_{f_{0}-{\Delta_{h}}}^{f_{0}+{\Delta_{h}}}|\widehat{x^{*}\cdot H}(f)|^{2}\mathrm{d}f\geq T\mathcal{N}^{2}/k,

where
\mathcal{N}^{2}\geq\delta\|x^{*}\|_{T}^{2}
.
Let
z^{*}(t):=(x^{*}\cdot H)*G^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)
and
z(t)=(x\cdot H)*{G}^{(j)}_{\sigma,b}(t)
.
Let
g_{z}(t):=z(t)-z^{*}(t)
. Let
U=\{t_{0}\in\mathbb{R}~|~H(t)>1-\delta_{1}~\forall t\in[t_{0},t_{0}+\beta]\}
. Suppose that
\|g_{z}(t)\|_{T}^{2}\leq c\|z^{*}(t)\|_{U}^{2}
, where
c\in(0,0.001)
is a small universal constant.

For
s\geq O(k\log(k)\log(1/\rho))
, let
S_{D_{U}}=\{t_{1},\dots,t_{s}\}
be a set of i.i.d. samples from the distribution
D_{U}
defined as Eq. (
52
). Let the weights
w_{i}=1/(TsD_{U}(t_{i}))
for
i\in[s]
.

Then, with probability at least
0.85
,

\|z(t)\|_{S_{D_{U}},w}^{2}\geq(0.2-20c)\cdot\|z^{*}(t)\|_{U}^{2}

Proof.

We consider the expectation of
\|g_{z}(t)\|_{S_{U},w}^{2}
first.

\displaystyle\E\Big[\sum_{j=1}^{s}w_{i}|g_{z}(t_{j})|^{2}\Big]=

\displaystyle~\sum_{j=1}^{s}\E_{t_{j}\sim D_{U}}[w_{i}|g_{z}(t_{j})|^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~\sum_{j=1}^{s}\E_{t_{j}\sim D_{U}}\Big[\frac{1}{TsD_{U}(t_{i})}|g_{z}(t_{j})|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq
